ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-senat-52-2.u252	Thank you very much, Mrs. Marshal. With regard to the question of Senator Libicki, I would like to say that this is a situation similar to that which arises in the event that the President makes a request to the Constitutional Tribunal within the framework of the so-called. preventative control, i.e. before the signing of the law, and the Sejm in the meantime, before the Court gives its ruling, will adopt provisions of the same wording, the President will sign them and these provisions will become the law in force. This was the case, among others. in 2015, when President Komorowski addressed to the Constitutional Tribunal the Act of 10 July 2015 amending the Act on the Protection of Monuments and certain other Acts. Before the Constitutional Tribunal, which had given its ruling in this case only last year, settled the case, there was the adoption of a law which implemented certain provisions of this very contested law addressed to the Constitutional Tribunal. The judgment was announced, a law was published which introduced these amendments. And it was analogical, you could say, situation. The legal system suddenly contains 2 provisions: one of this law, which has been challenged and the other of this law, which has been passed and signed in the meantime. And the constitution does not resolve such a situation when there is a collision related to the fact that, for example. The President has benefited from a preventive proposal, the Court has been considering this proposal for a long time, and in the meantime there has been the adoption of a law which partly or even fully covers the same range of cases. And the same is true of a veto. The President's request for review of the bill, the one who decides when such a proposal will be put to the vote, is the Marshal of the Sejm. Therefore, questions concerning when, under what circumstances, this will happen, should be addressed to the Marshal of the Sejm as the one who broadcasts all requests to the Sejm. Now I'm asking Senator Dowhan about the question about the age of retirement. Mr. Senator, at the beginning of my speech, I pointed out that this is a reference to the changes that apply to the law of the general courts. There, this limit to resting was set at 65 years for men and 60 years for women. In the case of the Supreme Court, this is the uniform limit of the age of retirement set at 65 years, and women only at their own request. I mean, only when they come out with an initiative. They may retire after the age of sixty. It seems to me that comparing Polish solutions with solutions in force in the United States is at least unauthorised. First of all, because of the so-called. the path to the profession of judge. In the United States this is the crowning of the legal profession, in Poland this path to becoming a professional is completely different. I in the Sejm quoted the statements of one of the representatives of the Polish Judges' Association, which indicated that she became a judge at the age of about 30 years, maybe about 10 years old, and is already so tired that she wants to maintain those provisions that talk about the possibility of early retirement by judges. Thus, a pragmatism indicates that because there is a different path to becoming a judge, otherwise these conditions may also be shaped, which justify a judge's transition to a resting state. And the solutions that are in force or are adopted in the presidential bill and this law already adopted by the Sejm refer to those which are in force in the law on the general court system from 1 October this year. I absolutely don't see any threat to the independence of the judge here. I emphasized this by answering your senators' questions in the context of the National Judicial Council Act. This independence is this inner independence, the independence of the judge who issues the ruling. And of course, as you said today, there are various forms of guaranteeing this independence, but how the judge is shaped, how his conscience is shaped, the legislator or even the appointing or evaluating judges have no influence. Thank you very much.

With regard to the senator's question on the issues which the provisions of the Act improve the situation from the perspective of the parties, participants in the proceedings or from a social perspective, it must be clearly pointed out that at least three legal, fundamental institutions, which are included in the Law on the Supreme Court, give such a socially desirable effect. This is primarily a matter of introducing jurors to the Supreme Court, which is precisely an element of the social factor, which is also a mapping of the provision of the article. 182 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which states that the participation of citizens in the exercise of justice is governed by a law. It is a very general constitutional provision, but indicating the direction that the legislator had in mind, recognising that this participation is desirable and necessary. For us, there is no doubt that the jury, whether it is a disciplinary judge or a party to the ruling, as regards the issue of the recognition of an emergency complaint, is also more confidence in the justice system, and precisely the introduction of an element of life experience, assuming that we are dealing with a mixed composition, which includes both professional judges and jurors. And this competence of life experience, very different, because this choice of jury will be made, it is an indication that in this regard disciplinary responsibility is needed, it is also an element of such social justice control, strengthening legitimacy and trust in justice, as well as ruling on disciplinary matters. Especially where we have a basis, also in an emergency complaint, where we are talking about mapping the need to maintain the rule of law and justice. And this social factor is one of these elements. Undoubtedly, the second element to be said about, which is closely related to the former, is the emergency complaint. The Minister has already spoken quite extensively on this subject, but it may be worth noting that in those cases where there is a final judgment that violates the principles or values expressed in the Constitution, where there is a gross violation of the law, or where there is an obvious contradiction of the evidence collected with the decision, there is a basis for such an evident error of the court to be removed, even when the appeal has not previously been granted and there is a situation of certain helplessness. This is a reconstruction of trust in the Polish justice system, but it is also an indication that the social factor will participate in the evaluation of these disciplinary delicacies. ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-senat-52-2.u282	Thank you very much, Mrs. Marshal. Senator, there are state offices for which the shortening of the term of office is precisely specified in the constitutional provisions. This is the case with regard to the Sejm and the Senate, in the case of the President of the Republic of Poland. You know perfectly well that the Senate's term of office can only be shortened in three situations, 2 of which are related to the adoption of an appropriate order by the President. The first case is where none of the procedures, i.e. de facto Article, has been established. The Commission shall adopt delegated acts in accordance with Article 108 (3) of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, in accordance with the procedure laid down in Article 108 (3) of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, in accordance with the procedure laid down in Article 108 (3) of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, in accordance with the procedure laid down in The second case, also in the hands of the President, is if, in due course, the budget bill is not passed and submitted to the President. The third case envisaged by the Constitution is the adoption of a resolution by the Sejm by a majority of 2/3 of the statutory composition to shorten its own term of office, but this is also followed by a shortening of the Senate term. In other cases, the constitution does not, on the contrary, provide even for an extension of the term of office if it were to end during a state of emergency. With regard to the office of President, as well as the senator knows perfectly well, these are enumerated in the Constitution cases where the President may end his term of office before the expiry of five years. One such case is the death of the President, another: the declaration of permanent incapacity of the President to serve by the National Assembly, the submission of office in accordance with the judgment of the Court of State or the resignation of the President, or the annulment of the election of the President by the Supreme Court. Because, as we know very well, this can happen, especially when there were early elections. The Supreme Court, according to the electoral code, has 30 days to investigate whether the President's election was important or not. If the President is sworn in before the end of this period which the Supreme Court has to examine the validity of elections, then, as you know, this term may expire earlier. And in the case of other offices, this constitutional regulation refers to the law. And, for example, The law on the Ombudsman regulates how and in what situations the Ombudsman may be revoked by the Sejm. Let me remind you that this is the majority 3/5 votes in favour. They may revoke the Ombudsman if he has embezzled his oath if he has become permanently incapable of exercising his office. It also regulates issues related to the appeal of the Act on the Ombudsman of the Rights of the Child, which says that he is being dismissed in certain situations by the Sejm with the consent of the Senate, or the Act on the Protection of Personal Data, which contains appropriate regulations with regard to the General Data Protection Officer. So here we need to distinguish between two categories of situations relating to shortening the term of office of the bodies that have been discussed in the Constitution. One group of bodies is those for which the constitution regulates the shortening of the term of office in a very precise way. The second group of bodies are those bodies for which the Constitution regulates the duration of the term of office, but the regulation on the circumstances in which this term may be shortened is referred to the laws. And, as I told Mr. Senator, I only mentioned a few examples of offices that could lead to early termination of my term of office. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-senat-52-2.u324	As regards matters relating to the justification of judgments, it is a provision of competence which gives the Supreme Court, in such a case where no justification has been drawn up, the possibility of requesting that justification. And if there is such a situation that there would be a need to reproduce the act, then there are legal institutions in the relevant civil proceedings. Of course, in the vast majority of cases, the justification for the judgment is available. As far as issues relating to the possibility of sending judges are concerned, I will say that anyone who has gone through the trouble of reading the bill will find the answer. There is such a possibility in the Act to delegate judges to the Supreme Court. This is directly from the bill. I'm sure you senators know the bill very well, so I won't repeat these circumstances. As far as Senator Komarnicki's question is concerned, I have just answered Senator Czarnobaj's question. I'm sorry you guys didn't listen to each other's answers, but I pointed out that we have no constitutional doubts. So, to the question is this true?I answer: no, it is not true, in our view there are no constitutional objections. [[Applause]]
